Smithian Fold Theory: A Zero-Axiom, Zero-Free-Parameter Derivation of the Fine-Structure Constant — Lean 4 Formal Verification

Abstract

Smithian Fold Theory (SFT) develops a structural theory of everything from its operational root, with zero added axioms and zero free scientific parameters. This cumulative preprint presents its native Lean formalisation, preserving the definitions, dependency order, derivations, complete candidate grammars, controls and empirical boundaries of the existing V3 model. The opening result is the exact terminal inverse fine-structure ratio, alpha^(-1) = 503846395469/3676744786. Its binary covers, typed structural blocks, finite direction-promotion ladder and exact rational assembly are derived before measurement; the corresponding Lean proofs have no axiom dependencies. The manuscript then develops the broader verified model, from the operational root, structural One, Count, Part and Fold through mathematics, information, computation and the completed physics results. This edition covers 191 completed entries of the 2,781-entry V3 census. The accompanying formalisation contains 9,211 audited theorems; the audit covers 20,782 declarations and reports zero axiom dependencies. Source correspondence, executable comparisons and chronological records accompany the manuscript. Successive versions will incorporate further completed derivations until the full model is formalised.

1. Opening result: the fine-structure constant

The opening result connects the structural foundation of SFT to an exact dimensionless ratio. The complete derivation is carried into native Lean from the pinned V3 model. Each numerical block retains its generated structural role; the terminal order is fixed by exhaustion of the three-direction promotion object.

Let b = 2 be the positive predecessor of the independently derived generator c = 3. The least complete binary covers of the carriers c^c = 27 and c^(c + 1) = 81 have depths 5 and 7. The independent decompositions give 5 = 2 + 3 and 7 = 3 + 4. These generate the tower 2^7 = 128, boundary 3^2 = 9 and complete cover 2 x 5^3 = 250.

Promoting each of the three interchangeable directions once generates the complete finite ladder 125, 175, 245, 343. Each step replaces one down-depth factor by an up-depth factor. After all three directions have been promoted, no successor is admitted. The first rung, 125, supplies the base cover 250 = 2 x 125 and hence the separate leading-order result; the terminal fourth-order assembly adds the reciprocal continuation built from the remaining rungs 175, 245 and 343. The terminal effective cover and inverse fine-structure ratio are:

D = 250 + 1 / (175 + 1 / (245 + 1 / 343))
alpha^(-1) = 128 + 9(D + 1)/D = 503846395469/3676744786
alpha = 3676744786/503846395469

The leading rung yields alpha^(-1) = 34259/250. The terminal fourth order follows from the complete generated ladder. It is not selected by a numerical fit. The source construction reads no measured value of alpha. Its separately registered empirical comparison retains the original post-seal boundary.

Native proofs establish the least-cover property, complete promotion stages, decreasing remaining-direction count, terminal closure and exact rational identities. All 1,024 registered candidate forms are retained, together with the three operational witnesses and four controls. Complete source-program comparisons retain candidate order, decisions, reasons, output fields and hashes. The entry is SFT-PHYS-CONSTANT-INVERSE-FINE-STRUCTURE-001; its nine registered dependencies were completed before it.

In this edition, zero axioms means the audited Lean declarations report an empty axiom-dependency set. Zero free scientific parameters means that the scientific construction uses the generated V3 quantities and original constraints, without fitted coefficients or measurement-fed choices. Host indices and explicit inputs of general finite operations retain their source roles. The exact expression above and its proof constitute the opening result of the wider manuscript.

2. A cumulative paper, released at scientific milestones

The paper develops SFT as a unified theory and grows with the native verification of the full V3 model. Alpha introduces the first release. Future opening results will feature the unified constants and further major derivations when their source entries are complete. Expanded editions are planned at 500 completed entries and approximately every 250 entries thereafter, with additional releases for substantial scientific milestones. Each edition incorporates all newly completed scientific material and identifies its corresponding source and verification snapshot.

The full scientific exposition follows in the same manuscript. The release includes the chronological timeline, coverage census, source correspondence, native Lean sources and retained verification evidence as separately accessible companion files. Development history belongs to the timeline; scientific definitions, derivations, methods and results belong to the paper.

The physics sections include Structural Generator Three, Spatial Dimension Three, Boundary Rank Two, Inverse Fine Structure, Charged-Lepton Cubic, Charged-Lepton Terminal and Electroweak Fold Mixing. The complete alpha construction is developed under "Inverse fine-structure cover construction" and "Terminal inverse fine-structure ratio".

The immutable scientific code checkpoint for this edition is b8f732515dd06e8251c06b1c45e3fdfb858b986d in MettaMazza/SFT-v4-LEAN-VERIFIED. The V3 model is pinned to 76f1b34e9e9682989adcbb088797b4ae4ce4fcf1 in MettaMazza/ernos-labs-sft-platform. Lean is pinned to leanprover/lean4:v4.35.0. COVERAGE.json identifies completed source entries; reference/CORRESPONDENCE.json maps translated sources. The retained TURN_271_ALPHA_COMPLETION.json records the alpha derivation components, and TURN_279_PARITY_REPORT.json records the latest aggregate build, candidate-output comparison and axiom audit. Entry completion and the count of individual theorems are reported separately.

To reproduce the repository checks, install the pinned Lean toolchain and run lake build, followed by python3 verification/check_current_parity.py from the repository root. The latter regenerates native output and performs the configured source-output and axiom checks. Source-specific completion reports retain the additional derivation and complete-program evidence.

5. Model and scope

The source model is Smithian Fold Theory V3, repository MettaMazza/ernos-labs-sft-platform, commit 76f1b34e9e9682989adcbb088797b4ae4ce4fcf1. Its census registers 2,781 model-admitted claims. The complete formalisation follows all 2,781 entries in dependency order, preserving their original definitions, derivations, declared constraints, controls and empirical boundaries. Each translated component is bound to its V3 source, and full-model verification requires both native proofs of the registered formal statements and correspondence of the associated executable procedures. Empirical records retain their original evidential status. The results presented here cover 191 completed entries: fifteen Foundation entries, twelve Mathematics entries, twelve Information Science entries and twelve Formal Computation entries and ten Computability entries and thirteen Computational Complexity entries and fourteen Algorithms entries and twelve Semantics entries and the 12 completed Distributed Computation entries (Fold concurrency law, Fold computational-causality law, Fold synchronization law, Fold communication law, Fold distributed partial-order law, Fold consensus law, Fold agreement-impossibility law, Fold replication and consistency law, Fold computational fault-model law, Fold distributed-knowledge law, Fold locality law, Fold network-computation law), plus the Physics Structural Generator Three, Spatial Dimension Three, Boundary Rank Two, Inverse Fine Structure, Charged-Lepton Cubic, Charged-Lepton Terminal and Electroweak Fold Mixing entries. Their finite-generation derivations and exact source operations are presented below. Full-entry verification remains distinct from the translated portions.

SFT distinguishes the structural One, generated positive finite counts and exact positive parts from host implementation indices. Its derivational constraints exclude added axioms, free or fitted scientific parameters, negative proof quantities, irrational or imaginary proof values, floating equality and completed infinity. Formalisation preserves the registered source boundary of each result.

6. Operational root

Let R be the registered challenge-record domain and P(r) its presentation flag. Write U for unpresented-absence and O for presented-occurrence. Classification and acceptance are
  κ(r)=U if P(r)=false; κ(r)=O if P(r)=true,
  A(r)=P(r).
The Boolean cases are exhaustive, so for every r∈R,
  A(r)=true ⇔ κ(r)=O,
  κ(r)=κ(s) ⇔ P(r)=P(s).
In the generated grammar G=[U,O], A(U)=false and A(O)=true. Filtering G therefore gives [O], proving both existence and uniqueness of the survivor. Deleting O gives filter_A([U])=[], while the modified acceptance A′(U)=A′(O)=true gives filter_A′(G)=[U,O]. Since U≠O, the modified survivor set is not unique. The typed boundary concerns these operational presentation records; neither identifier nor descriptive text enters P.

The registered operational challenge partition has exactly two classes: unpresented-absence and presented-occurrence. An unpresented challenge supplies no counterexample. A presented challenge supplies an occurrence and is therefore not nothing at the operational boundary.

The decision accepts precisely presented-occurrence. Exhaustive enumeration establishes that both registered classes occur in the candidate list. The unique-survivor theorem establishes that a candidate is accepted if and only if it is presented-occurrence. No premise about a particular presentation's internal content is introduced.

Within the registered partition, deleting the presented witness leaves no accepted candidate. The decision is definitionally the presentation predicate. Three additional native proofs establish the false-premise, tampered-artifact and fixed-boundary control outcomes. The source census, decisions, closure evidence and all four control records are emitted by native Lean definitions; every stored field and digest agrees with the pinned V3 artifacts.

The tampered-artifact premise is represented by an acceptance function that accepts both source classes. Its survivor set is exactly the original two-element candidate list. Constructive proofs establish that the two survivors are distinct and that no unique survivor exists. These statements formalise the source rejection condition while retaining the original control record for exact artifact correspondence.

The record-level formalisation retains exactly candidate identity, presentation flag and descriptive form from the source dictionary. Classification is derived from the presentation flag. For every record in this schema, its classified candidate has the same presentation status; classifying a generated representative returns that representative. Two records have the same class if and only if their presentation flags agree. Thus every such record maps into the registered exhaustive partition, independently of its identifier or descriptive text.

The source decision agrees with this classification for every record. Acceptance is equivalent to the presented-occurrence class and to the source operational presentation witness; an accepted record cannot be unpresented. Changing either metadata string changes neither acceptance nor its reason. Deleting presentation rejects the record. These statements formalise the source record decision and operational presentation relation, while preserving all three source fields and the generated evidence values.

The complete registered Root entry is formalised: its source definitions, operational derivation and closure statements, exhaustive candidate partition, four controls and program methods are represented. Fresh native execution reproduces the pinned census, decisions, closure, control records and their hashes; registration fields and refusal of unknown identifiers agree. The source program declares no prior dependencies, axioms or free scientific parameters. Fifteen foundation entries, twelve mathematics entries, twelve information-science entries and sixty-five computation entries and seven Physics entries establish one hundred and eleven completed source entries out of 2,781; the 8,573 audited Lean theorems count individual results across the existing translated work.

Source claim: SFT-ROOT-THERE-IS-NO-NOTHING.

7. Structural One

Let C={none, proper, complete} denote root coverage and E={no-extra, has-extra} denote added coverage. The source relation domain is C×E, with 3·2=6 classes. Its survival predicate is
  S(c,e) ⇔ (c=complete) ∧ (e=no-extra).
For c=none or c=proper the first conjunct fails; for e=has-extra the second fails. The pair (complete,no-extra) satisfies both, hence
  {(c,e)∈C×E : S(c,e)} = {(complete,no-extra)}.
This singleton is exact-self-whole. For every source record r, the classification map retains precisely its two relations, so S(r)⇔κ(r)=(complete,no-extra). Metadata changes preserve both relations and therefore preserve the decision. These relations express structural wholeness before any numerical magnitude is assigned.

A representation of the root occurrence is classified by its coverage—none, proper or complete—and by whether it adds unforced material. The product gives six classes: omitted-root, replacement-extra, fragmented-root, fragment-plus-extra, exact-self-whole and whole-plus-extra, in source enumeration order.

The representation survives precisely when its coverage is complete and it contains no extra material. Thus exact-self-whole is the unique surviving class. Omission, fragmentation and addition are rejected by the same ordered conditions as in V3. The One is structural self-wholeness, not a numerical magnitude introduced at this stage.

The record-level derivation uses the three source root-coverage relations and two source extra-coverage relations. Their generated product is proved identical, in order, to the six stored candidates. Classification retains both relations and is inverted by the generated representatives. The four source record fields, survival decision and ordered reason all agree with the generated records.

For every record in that relation domain, acceptance is equivalent to complete coverage together with no extra material, and hence to the exact-self-whole class. Replacing coverage by none or proper rejects the record; setting the extra relation to has-extra also rejects it. Acceptance and its reason are unchanged by arbitrary identifier or descriptive-text changes. These universal record statements formalise the source omission, fragmentation, addition and metadata-independence arguments without introducing a numerical magnitude.

The complete One source program preserves its registered dependency, all six ordered candidates, actual identifier-based decisions, closure and generality records, and four original controls. Native lookup correctness and the non-hash control outcomes are proved. Fresh execution matches every corresponding pinned field and digest, including refusal of an unknown identifier. Together with the general derivation above, this completes the registered entry.

Source claim: SFT-FOUNDATION-ONE-001. Registered dependency: the operational root.

8. Exact positive finite count

Let B be the base generation trace with its sole occurrence position o. For an extension of trace t, write lift for the embedding of prior positions and ν for the fresh terminal, with ν outside the image of lift. Define complete retention recursively by
  R(B)=[o],
  R(extend(t))=map(lift,R(t)) ++ [ν].
The base is nonempty and contains its generated position exactly once. If R(t) contains every generated position exactly once, injectivity of lift preserves those occurrences and their uniqueness; freshness of ν prevents a repetition when it is appended. Thus induction establishes nonemptiness, complete coverage and exact-once retention at every finite extension.

For a proposed finite retention L, let m_L(x) be the number of occurrences of generated position x, and Extra(L) indicate an outside-trace occurrence. The admitted condition is
  (∀x generated by t, m_L(x)=1) ∧ ¬Extra(L).
Deleting a generated x makes m_L(x)=0; duplicating it makes m_L(x)>1; an outside addition makes Extra(L) true. Each modification therefore fails one explicit conjunct. Retaining only map(lift,R(t)) at an extension fails at ν. The grammar count is 2+2·2·2=10: two extra-material cases with no coverage, and two coverage cases (proper or complete), two multiplicities and two extra-material cases. Its unique admitted class is complete, once, no-extra.

V3 defines positive finite count through the complete generation trace of a nonempty finite succession of structural-One occurrences. Every generated occurrence is retained once and no ungenerated occurrence is added.

Its representation grammar has ten classes. With no coverage, multiplicity is not applicable and the two extra-material classes remain. With proper or complete coverage, multiplicity is once or duplicated, independently paired with absent or present extra material. Only complete-coverage__once__no-extra survives. The formal classification retains this exact domain, including the absence of a multiplicity choice in the no-coverage case.

The native theorems establish complete enumeration and unique survival within this registered grammar. No replacement numerical count model is required for these results.

The base/successor argument is encoded as finite generation syntax: a structural-One base followed by extensions, each retaining prior occurrence positions and introducing a fresh terminal. Positions denote generated occurrence identities within the trace. They are not interpreted as a numerical magnitude. The complete representation is constructed by retaining the base and appending each newly generated terminal once.

Structural induction proves that every generated position occurs in the complete trace, that no position is repeated, and that the trace is nonempty. The previous complete trace is proper coverage of an extension: all its positions remain, but the fresh terminal is absent. Appending that terminal restores completeness. Repeating any generated position violates exact multiplicity; removing a position loses complete coverage. The two source complete-generation clauses determine a unique trace identity at every finite extension. Finite proposed representations are then classified by their retained generated positions and presence of ungenerated material. The latter is represented by the source outside-trace relation; extra-object content is not a criterion of the source classifier.

Constructive position equality and membership establish the semantic connection to the ten-class grammar. Complete coverage is equivalent to retaining every generated position. The multiplicity predicate is equivalent to absence of repeated retained positions. The extra-material predicate is equivalent to the presence of an ungenerated occurrence. For every finite proposal, survival is therefore equivalent to complete exact-once retention with no ungenerated material. Canonical complete proposals survive; any missing position, repeated retained position or outside addition is rejected. The previous complete prefix of an extension is rejected because it omits the new terminal.

Execution comparisons cover 493 proposals over one-, two- and three-occurrence traces, reaching all ten source classes and matching their decisions and reasons. These bounded comparisons supplement the universal Lean equivalence; they do not replace it.

The complete Count source program preserves its registered dependency, all ten ordered candidates, actual identifier-based decisions, closure and generality records, and four original controls. Native lookup correctness and the non-hash control outcomes are proved. Fresh execution matches every corresponding pinned field and digest, including refusal of an unknown identifier. Together with the general derivation above, this completes the registered entry.

Source claim: SFT-FOUNDATION-COUNT-001. Registered dependency: the structural One.

9. Exact positive part coordinate

Let W be the nonempty generated fibre trace of the registered partition and H(x) the held-selection predicate. The selected trace is S=filter_H(W). For every fibre x,
  x∈S ⇔ x∈W ∧ H(x).
Consequently S⊆W, and filtering cannot duplicate a fibre absent repetition in W. A witness x∈W with H(x) makes S nonempty. At the host-record level this gives 0<length(S)≤length(W). The coordinate retains the generation records themselves:
  coord(S,W)=(S,W),  π₁(coord(S,W))=S,  π₂(coord(S,W))=W.
If a coordinate has these same two projections, ordered-pair equality identifies it with (S,W). The registered admission condition is the conjunction of complete partition coverage, disjointness, equal fibres, an internal held selection, presence of the held record, presence of the whole record and absence of an extra coordinate. Failure of any one conjunct rejects the form. No cross-partition equivalence follows at this stage; that relation is derived in the subsequent common-refinement construction.

The Part derivation registers a finite partition of the structural One and a held selection. Its seven relation domains classify partition coverage, overlap, fibre equality, selection relation, held-count presence, whole-count presence and unforced-extra-coordinate presence. Their source-ordered product contains 192 forms. A native theorem establishes that acceptance is equivalent to all seven requirements: complete, disjoint, equal-fibred, within-partition, both counts present and no extra coordinate. Exactly that form survives. Removing either count, omitting whole coverage, allowing overlap or unequal fibres, using an empty or outside selection, or adding an unforced coordinate is proved to fail.

For a registered whole’s finite generated fibre trace, a held predicate constructs the selected trace. Constructive proofs establish containment and absence of repeated fibres. A witnessed held fibre makes the selection nonempty. Its host-record length cannot exceed the whole trace length; this expresses the finite containment bound without installing a new magnitude in the model. The exact coordinate is the pair of held and whole generation records. Its projections retain those two identities, and the pair is unique from them. Both records are nonempty for a witnessed selection.

The complete Part source program retains the original One and Count dependencies, all 192 candidates, every decision payload, closure and generality certificate, and four controls. Fresh actual identifier-based execution reproduces every pinned field and digest and refuses unknown identifiers. The original boundary control retains the source statement that cross-partition equivalence belongs to a later derivation. Together with the general coordinate proofs, these results complete the registered Part entry.

Source claim: SFT-FOUNDATION-PART-001. Registered dependencies: structural One and Count. Equivalence of coordinates from different partitions is not assumed here; it is the subject of the separately registered common-refinement derivation.

10. Generated common-refinement pairing

For generated fibre traces A and B, write A⊠B for the ordered list [(a,b): a∈A, b∈B], with A traversed first and B second. Held selections H_A⊆A and H_B⊆B lift to
  L_A=H_A⊠B,  L_B=A⊠H_B.
Every lifted cell belongs to A⊠B, since both its origins belong to the corresponding whole traces. If these two lifted lists have equal length n, the generated index pairing sends the i-th cell of L_A to the i-th cell of L_B for each 0≤i<n. Its inverse sends the i-th cell back. Thus g(f(x))=x and f(g(y))=y; injectivity follows by applying g to f(x)=f(x′), and onto coverage follows from y=f(g(y)). Unequal lengths cannot supply this complete index pairing.

Identity uses f(x)=x, reversal exchanges f and g, and chaining uses f₂∘f₁ with inverse g₁∘g₂. Under a shared descendant trace D, the refinement map is (x,d)↦(f(x),d), with inverse (y,d)↦(g(y),d). Both inverse identities follow by substitution. In the one-of-two/two-of-four example, the selected lifts have lengths 1·4=2·2=4; the order pairing therefore covers both lifts completely.

The common-refinement construction generates cells by traversing each source A label and then each source B label. The selected A lift retains all B descendants of each held A label; the selected B lift retains all A origins of each held B label. Every generated cell has source origins, and each pair of source origins occurs in the construction. The executable label formatting and order are preserved exactly.

The registered generated-bijection procedure pairs finite host indices in order and checks equal trace lengths and bounds. Native proofs establish that the bounds always hold, that acceptance is equivalent to equal finite trace lengths, and that every index on both accepted sides occurs in the generated pairing. A constructive pairing supplies forward and inverse maps with both inverse identities; injectivity and onto coverage follow. Identity, reversal and chaining establish the registered pairing laws. Uniform refinement retains the shared descendant identity and preserves both inverse identities. For the concrete pair-cell procedure, equal selected-trace lengths remain equal after appending the same descendant trace by nested generation. This is proved structurally without assuming a multiplication law. The source one-of-two/two-of-four example accepts; the unequal source example rejects.

Source claim: SFT-FOUNDATION-PART-EQUIVALENCE-001. Dependencies: One, Count and Part. The exact nine-domain product has 1,024 forms. A constructive product-membership proof establishes exhaustive enumeration. The source identifier-equality decision is proved equivalent to all nine witness requirements; exactly the registered witness survives. Eleven adverse relation cases are proved rejected. Complete selected-origin lifting is proved contained in the common refinement, and the accepted generated pairing is both one-to-one and onto on the lifted trace positions. The complete original program and its evidence records match the pinned V3 source outputs.

11. Minimal Count extension in Fold

Write B for the base Count stage and S(t) for its extension. Define u≼t when u is a generated prefix of t. A minimal nonidentity stage t satisfies
  t≠B and ∀u≼t, (u≠B ⇒ u=t).
Every t≠B is an extension and contains S(B) as a prefix. Substitution u=S(B) into minimality forces t=S(B). Conversely the only prefixes of S(B) are B and S(B), so S(B) satisfies the condition. Therefore
  minimal_nonidentity(t) ⇔ t=S(B).
Its generated positions are precisely lift(o) and ν, with lift(o)≠ν by freshness. The complete retained trace is [lift(o),ν]. A later stage S(S(t)) has a proper nonidentity prefix S(B), so cannot be minimal; B fails nonidentity itself.

Fold classifies a generated Count stage as identity, first extension or later extension. Structural proofs identify those classes exactly with the base, its immediate successor and a successor of a successor. The generated-stage prefix relation retains each earlier stage under further extension. Every nonidentity finite trace contains the first extension. The first extension is nonidentity and every nonidentity prefix of it is itself; it is therefore minimal. Conversely, any minimal nonidentity stage must equal that first extension. Identity and every later finite extension are consequently excluded as minimal Fold stages.

The first extension retains the base occurrence and the appended occurrence. Its generated-position domain is exhausted by these two distinct positions, and its complete record retains each once. This formalises the source first-extension count argument without introducing signed numerical labels, a fitted quantity or a maximum finite depth. The source equality, disjointness, whole-preservation, distinct-label and return relations are retained in the complete grammar and characterized by the stage acceptance theorem below.

The seven source relation domains generate 288 forms in the original order. The native decision retains the exact source conjunction and ordered explanations. Exhaustive membership, unique survival and acceptance iff all seven requirements are established. Nine adverse criterion values are rejected. A source-record adapter derives the extension field from the actual Count stage and preserves the other six fields. Its acceptance is equivalent to minimal nonidentity of that stage together with complete whole coverage, disjoint and equal fibres, distinct-held labels, present return and absence of extra data. Identity and every later stage are rejected for every choice of the remaining source fields.

The complete Fold program preserves all 288 original candidates and the One, Count and Part dependencies. Fresh native execution matches every census field, actual identifier-based decision, trace/proof digest, closure/generality certificate and original control result. Unknown identifiers are refused. These results accompany the general minimal-stage derivation and source-relation acceptance equivalence, completing the registered Fold entry.

Source claim: SFT-FOUNDATION-FOLD-001. Dependencies: One, Count and Part.

12. Complete finite Fold-word assembly and return

Let a and b denote the two distinct held labels and ε the empty structural word. Define the ordered supports by
  W₀=[ε],
  W_(n+1)=concat_{w∈W_n}[w·a,w·b].
A successor w·x retains its prefix w and final label x; removing that final label gives ρ(w·x)=w. If w·x=v·y then terminal-label equality gives x=y and prefix equality gives w=v. Together with a≠b this proves the two branches are disjoint and preserves uniqueness inductively.

Induction on n proves
  w∈W_n ⇔ length(w)=n and every label of w belongs to {a,b}.
At n=0 the only such word is ε. At n+1, separating the final label yields a length-n prefix satisfying the induction hypothesis and one of the two admitted labels, hence exactly one generated branch. Conversely each generated branch has the asserted length and label membership. The host-record cardinalities obey |W₀|=1 and |W_(n+1)|=2|W_n|, hence |W_n|=2^n. This count follows from complete recursive generation, not a premise replacing it.

The assembly construction begins with the structural empty One word. Each successor appends held-a and held-b, in that order, to every preceding word. The nonnegative extension index is the source host recursion input; the empty word is a structural form. Negative host extension inputs are rejected.

Appending a held label retains both the predecessor word and the last label. The return relation recovers the predecessor by removing precisely the appended terminal label. Both distinct branches return to the same predecessor, and a successor has only one such predecessor. These properties hold at every finite generated depth. Appending different labels or extending different predecessor words produces distinct successor words; induction establishes uniqueness of every complete support.

A word belongs to the support after n extensions if and only if it has host-record length n and every label belongs to the source pair. This universal equivalence establishes completeness and absence of additional words. Every retained successor has a return to the preceding support. An outside label or incorrect length excludes a proposed word; repeating a generated word violates uniqueness, and omitting one violates complete coverage. These statements are established by constructive finite-list and generation induction without an imported exponentiation law or a maximum depth.

Source claim: SFT-FOUNDATION-FOLD-ASSEMBLY-001. Dependencies: Count and Fold. The source keeps this recursive assembly claim separate from minimal Fold. Its seven relation domains produce 192 source-ordered forms. Exhaustive enumeration, equivalence of the original identifier decision with the seven criteria, unique survival and rejection of eight adverse values are established. The complete original program and its evidence records match the pinned V3 source outputs.

The canonical assembly retains every stage from the empty source word through the final finite depth. Each transition explicitly retains predecessor, appended label and successor. Its successor uniquely determines that transition. Every generated child at every earlier level has both its transition and return recorded in the full trace. Conversely, each stored stage, transition and return arises from the source construction; each return recovers its recorded predecessor.

A native proof certificate establishes the seven accepted relation requirements on this canonical construction: complete stage coverage, consistent lengths, complete support, word uniqueness, transition retention, return retention and absence of added records. The accepted source witness is realised at every positive finite depth. This result concerns the canonical construction. Proposed word supports additionally admit the executable semantic classification below; source coverage, transition and return relations remain independent inputs.

For an arbitrary finite proposed word support, executable membership and universal finite-list tests determine the source length, support-completeness, uniqueness and extra-word relations. A native theorem establishes acceptance exactly when the coverage relation is complete, every proposed word has the required length, every generated word is retained, no word is repeated, transitions and returns are present, every proposed word is generated and no extra rule is supplied. The coverage, transition and return fields are preserved as independent source relations; their truth is not inferred from a word list alone.

The source finite-set operations are represented constructively. Duplicate removal preserves precisely the original members and produces a duplicate-free list. Equality of the original length and this finite-set cardinality is equivalent to uniqueness. Two word sets compare equal exactly when their membership predicates agree. The original four assembly controls retain their predicates and serialized result hashes; the depth-one-through-five uniqueness/length check follows from the native all-finite theorem. The source literal boundary text check remains distinct from semantic exclusion results.

The finite assembly record representation includes predecessor–label–successor transitions and child–parent returns. Acceptance is equivalent to retention of every required transition and return, exclusion of ungenerated records, complete source coverage, correct word lengths, complete word support, word uniqueness and absence of extra words or rules. Canonical records satisfy these conditions. Missing required transitions, missing required returns and incorrect returns are rejected. Coverage remains the explicit source relation. Registration, census, decisions and closure are represented with the original source fields and ordering; candidate lookup uses the source identifier.

For positive integers n,d, define the retained numerator recursively by c_d(n)=n when n≤d, and c_d(n)=c_d(n−d) when n>d. In the latter branch 0<n−d<n, so the recursion terminates. Induction along the removals establishes 0<c_d(n)≤d and n=c_d(n)+kd for a nonnegative host removal count k. If r,r′∈{1,…,d} represent the same residue modulo d, then their difference has magnitude below d and is divisible by d, hence r=r′. This proves uniqueness of the retained representative. It follows that c_d(n+d)=c_d(n) and c_d(kd)=d for every positive whole-turn count k. The terminal representative of a complete turn is d/d, the One.

The exact casting operation acts on a positive count ratio n/d by removing one complete denominator while the numerator exceeds it. A structural recursion bound derived from n suffices for every positive d. A native removal-trace theorem proves termination and a unique retained numerator r with 0 < r ≤ d. Admitted numerators remain fixed; adding a complete denominator to a positive numerator does not change its retained value. Every positive whole number of complete turns retains d, representing the One. These results apply to all finite positive count pairs. The numerator-level result supplies the casting stage of the normalized primitive operations below.

The Fraction admission map preserves the reduced numerator and denominator exactly. An admitted part maps back to the same Fraction, and admission yields that part if and only if the original Fraction is its reduced-pair representation. Zero, signed values and values above the One are rejected; every reduced positive pair through the One is admitted. For admitted a and b, the strict Take guard is the cross-product inequality between their count pairs. Its difference numerator is positive and strictly smaller than the product denominator; equal and reversed Take inputs are rejected. Self-junction followed by cast has a positive numerator no larger than its denominator, and a complete One is Fold-fixed. The normalized operations reduce their output count pairs to the canonical Fraction representation.

For a positive pair (n,d), let g=gcd(n,d) and N(n,d)=(n/g,d/g). Divisibility by g gives positive integral quotients, and any common divisor of both quotients would enlarge the removed common factor; therefore gcd(n/g,d/g)=1. The identity (n/g)d=n(d/g) preserves the ratio exactly. For admitted parts a=n/d and b=m/e, the strict Take guard a>b is ne>md, and the retained difference is
  Take(a,b)=N(ne−md,de).
Because 0<ne−md<de for admitted inputs satisfying the guard, the result is a positive part below the One. Equality ne=md and reversal ne<md fail the guard. Self-junction and Fold retain their source order:
  Junction(a,a)=N(2n,d),
  Fold(a)=normalize(cast(Junction(a,a))).
Unison is equivalent to ne=md, the exact input cross-product equality.

Canonical Fraction construction divides the numerator and denominator by their greatest common divisor. Constructive proofs identify the native GCD computation, establish its divisibility and greatest-common-divisor properties, and show that the reduced quotient pair is coprime. Both quotients are positive, reduction preserves exact cross-product value, and an admitted input remains through the One. Normalization is stable on an already reduced part. The integer-pair constructor retains the source rejection messages for signed, zero and beyond-One inputs.

Fold first reduces the self-junction Fraction, then casts complete Ones and reduces the retained part. Guarded Take constructs and reduces its exact positive difference. Both operations return reduced admitted parts. Unison normalizes the quotient and compares it to the One; a native theorem makes this equivalent to equality of the input cross-products. Every part is in unison with itself, and the complete One is Fold-fixed. The proof arguments apply to all finite positive count pairs and use no denominator bound.

The Exact Operations grammar contains the complete 128-form product of domain, full-turn representation, Fold composition, Take guard, unison identity, trace custody and added-data classes. Enumeration is exhaustive; the source identifier decision is equivalent to all seven required criteria, and exactly the registered survivor satisfies them. Each adverse class is rejected. The trace-custody relation remains an explicit source criterion.

The positive Fraction cast adapter rejects zero and signed inputs and returns the normalized cast result for every positive reduced input. The four original controls retain their predicates: reversed Take rejection, changed-source identity, casting two complete Ones to the One, and the four specified hostile carrier constructions. Their full output fields and hashes agree with the pinned source. The eight literal closure fields are preserved.

The first Fold has the two positions already established by minimal Count extension. Every held selection is empty, one of the two singletons, or the whole pair. Its nonempty coordinate classes are precisely one-of-two and One. Every proper nonempty selection therefore normalizes to half-One. Exchanging the two positions is involutive and preserves the selection cardinality and exact coordinate.

Let x=n/d be reduced and positive. Junction(x,x)=One implies 2n=d. Then gcd(n,d)=gcd(n,2n)=n, so reducedness forces n=1 and consequently d=2. Conversely x=1/2 has self-junction 1. Thus
  Junction(x,x)=One ⇔ x=1/2.
Complement gives 1−1/2=1/2, while Fold(1/2)=cast(1)=1. The complete One is Fold-fixed because Fold(1)=cast(2)=1.

For every admitted reduced positive part, normalized self-junction is the One if and only if the part is half-One. The proof uses the full positive numerator/denominator domain: self-junction forces the denominator to be twice the numerator, while reducedness forces the numerator to be one. Half-One complements to itself and Folds to the One; the One is Fold-fixed. At half-One, complement and half-One phase translation have different values. These results formalize the registered complete-partition argument without a sampled rational search.

Source claim: SFT-FOUNDATION-HALF-ONE-001. Dependencies: minimal Fold, Exact Operations and Part Equivalence.

For an arbitrary admitted image y, the lower generated Fold preimage is the normalized ratio y/2. Its doubled numerator does not exceed its denominator, and native Fold of this preimage is exactly y. Phase translation is implemented as normalized addition of half-One followed by cast; the generated preimage pair retains the source lower and translated-upper definitions. The exact orbit one-of-three to two-of-three and back to one-of-three is proved using the native Fold operation. Its two values are distinct. Two steps return to the source, while every positive step count below two fails to return. Source claim: SFT-FOUNDATION-FOLD-DYNAMICS-001; dependencies: Exact Operations and Half-One.

Positive scaling of a count-pair representation scales its complete-One removal trace and retained numerator. These identities preserve the original operation while allowing its arbitrary-rational branch equations to be proved.

Write C for casting to the positive rational circle (0,1] and F(x)=C(2x). Define phase T(x)=C(x+1/2). For 0<x≤1/2, T(x)=x+1/2; for 1/2<x≤1, T(x)=x−1/2. In both branches a second application returns x, so T(T(x))=x. Doubling either branch changes 2x by exactly one complete turn, hence F(T(x))=F(x).
For any 0<y≤1, the two generated inverse images are
  L(y)=y/2,  U(y)=(y+1)/2.
They satisfy 0<L(y)≤1/2<U(y)≤1 and
  F(L(y))=C(y)=y,  F(U(y))=C(y+1)=y.
Conversely if F(x)=y, the first-half branch gives 2x=y and x=L(y); the second-half branch gives 2x−1=y and x=U(y). Therefore
  F(x)=y ⇔ x=L(y) or x=U(y),
with L(y)≠U(y). At y=1 the pair is (1/2,1), retaining the complete-turn representative at the endpoint.

For a part in the first half-circle, half-One phase translation has the exact normalized coordinate (twice the numerator plus the denominator)/(twice the denominator). Doubling this translated coordinate adds one complete One before cast, so it shares the original Fold image. Applying this result to the generated lower preimage proves that the generated upper preimage also Folds to every admitted image. The upper lies strictly beyond the half-circle boundary, while the lower lies on or below it; hence the two generated preimages are distinct. This establishes validity and distinctness of the generated pair. Every preimage is one of these two forms: a first-half part is recovered as the lower preimage of its Fold image, while a second-half part translates to the first half and is recovered using phase involution. Thus, for every admitted p and y, Fold(p) = y if and only if p is y/2 or cast(y/2 plus half-One). Distinctness and this exhaustive equivalence establish the exact two-preimage law over the entire positive rational circle.

The phase laws hold throughout the positive rational circle, including both half-circle branches and One. Complete-One removal is preserved under the affine half-One shift and under doubling. Consequently Fold(phase(x)) = Fold(x) and phase(phase(x)) = x for every admitted part x. The proofs retain the source normalization and cast order and remove the complete One introduced by two successive half-One translations.

For positive n define D_n=[1/n,2/n,…,n/n], with each coordinate normalized. For 1≤i≤n,
  F(i/(2n))=i/n,
  F((i+n)/(2n))=C(i/n+1)=i/n.
Every index 1≤j≤2n lies either at j=i or at j=i+n for exactly one such i, so the complete image has precisely the members of D_n. Cross multiplication gives i/n<j/n exactly when i<j. Thus D_n is strictly ordered, has n distinct entries and ends at n/n=One. Adjacent differences are
  (i+1)/n−i/n=1/n.
Sorting and removing duplicate image values therefore yields exactly D_n; at n=1 this is [One].

The sorted distinct Fold image is implemented over the exact reduced-rational carrier. Cross-product identity removes repeated values, and cross-product order determines the ascending output. 13. Formal representation and results

Each Lean candidate constructor denotes exactly one registered V3 candidate record. These constructors are encodings of the existing finite grammar. Field projections retain source text and field values. Decision functions implement the original branch predicates and rejection order.

For each translated grammar, the formalisation proves:

(a) Every candidate belongs to the enumerated domain.
(b) Acceptance is equivalent to equality with the source survivor.
(c) The source survivor is accepted.

The proofs use constructor elimination, reflexivity and explicit list membership. Lean reports no axiom dependencies for all 991 theorems. These results establish the registered finite classifications; they do not stand in for the remaining source closure arguments, control records or admission procedures.

On the annotated registration domain, passing also entails empty axiom and free-parameter lists and no missing dependencies under the source authority lookup. The typed Root registration serializes identically to its source record. Registration diagnostic order is checked on 272 cases.

The admission engine’s forcing gate preserves four ordered requirements: decision identifiers match the generated census in order; exactly one decision survives; every decision provides an explicit reason; every proof identity has canonical SHA-256 syntax. On the source-typed Candidate and CandidateDecision domains, a constructive theorem establishes that an empty violation list implies a survivor list of length one. The original Root decisions have one survivor, while marking both decisions as surviving produces two and is proved to be rejected by this gate. This connects the source tampering premise to the engine’s actual rejection condition.

Closure evidence retains its source scope, exact boundary, minimality and uniqueness flags, proof identity and optional generality identity. Within the source-typed field domains, passing the closure gate requires a nonblank boundary and successful minimality and uniqueness flags; depth-independent closure also requires a canonical SHA-256 generality identity. The control gate retains the required-kind, duplicate-kind, outcome, behavior-text and receipt-identity checks in source order. Passing implies that every supplied control has passed. Root closure with minimality removed or with its generality identity absent, and Root controls with failed status flags, are constructively proved to be rejected. These are engine-gate properties; they remain distinct from proving the complete operational-boundary semantics.

The candidate census retains the source generation rule, grammar boundary, signed expected cardinality, completeness identity and ordered candidate records. The census gate preserves the eight source checks and their diagnostic order. Passing requires nonblank generation and boundary text, a positive expected cardinality, matching candidate count and no duplicated identifiers. A constructive witness expresses each passing expected cardinality as a natural successor. Root censuses with an incorrect registered count or duplicated candidate are proved rejected. Executable correspondence covers 1,440 census variants, including negative and zero counts, Unicode identifiers, duplicated identifiers, blank forms and malformed identities, together with three direct Root cases.

17. Discrete collections and held selections

Write C=[c₁,…,c_n] for the ordered source collection. Admission requires n>0 and c_i≠c_j whenever i≠j. For a held list H, successful selection requires ∀h∈H, h∈C. If H=[] the result is the structural empty-One form; otherwise the result retains H itself. The complete ordered pair support is
  P(A,B)=concat_{a∈A}[(a,b):b∈B].
List induction gives (a,b)∈P(A,B) ⇔ a∈A ∧ b∈B. Thus every admitted held relation R⊆P(A,B) retains both origins of each pair. A fresh successor is C′=C++[x] with x∉C; pairwise distinctness of C plus freshness proves distinctness of C′. For a source a, define Out_R(a) as the retained list of pairs whose first endpoint is a. The source one-image condition is ∀a∈A, length(Out_R(a))=1. This counts pair occurrences, including any repetition, rather than replacing the list by a set.

For finite tuple-of-string forms, generated collections retain their exact input order and reject an empty collection or repeated forms. A successful held selection retains only members of its source collection; the native membership procedure is equivalent to list membership. An empty held selection returns the structural empty-One form, whereas a nonempty selection returns the tuple of held forms. Generated relations retain their accepted pair sequence within the complete Cartesian pair support. Fresh successor construction appends the new form and rejects an already retained member. The total-single-valued predicate counts exactly one outgoing pair for each source member and is true on an empty source. The implementation preserves these source guards, including the distinction between an empty collection and a collection containing an empty form. These results concern the discrete operations; the registered candidate grammar and the remaining general discrete laws are separately tracked.

For arbitrary finite source collections, membership of an ordered pair in complete pair support is equivalent to membership of its two endpoints in their respective collections. Every pair in a successful generated relation therefore has an origin in the left collection and a target in the right. The total-single-valued predicate is equivalent to an exact singleton condition on the retained outgoing pair list for every source member; repeated pairs remain counted as repeated occurrences. Fresh succession preserves collection uniqueness, and appending a fresh form to an accepted generated collection yields another accepted generated collection. These proofs apply at arbitrary finite size by structural induction, without a depth cutoff.

The discrete candidate grammar enumerates the complete product of eight binary questions: collection identity, canonical identity, membership, pair-support relations, map law, generation order, induction closure and additional constructors. All 256 proposals are represented. Exhaustive membership and unique-survivor proofs establish exactly the source all-admitted coordinate, with each rejected coordinate incompatible with acceptance. Every source identifier, exact form, decision and ordered rejection reason agrees under fresh execution. The six original operational witnesses retain their source constructions and predicates. The closure record and all four original control records agree with pinned V3, including one actual enumerated survivor and 256 distinct candidate identifiers. These results preserve the registered finite grammar and supplement the general collection, relation and succession proofs.

18. Finite collection induction

Let Generated(C) mean that C is obtained by a singleton base [x] and steps C↦C++[y] with y∉C. The forward induction establishes
  Generated(C) ⇒ C≠[] and C has no repeated members.
Conversely, split any admitted nonempty list into its prefix and final member. A singleton supplies the base. For a longer list, the prefix is nonempty and distinct, and the final member is absent from it; induction constructs the prefix and then the fresh append. Hence Generated(C) is equivalent to the source admission condition. For any property Q, the two premises
  ∀x, Q([x]),
  ∀C,y, Generated(C) ∧ Q(C) ∧ y∉C ⇒ Q(C++[y])
therefore imply Q(C) for every admitted C. No bound on the finite list length enters this argument.

The accepted domain of the discrete collection operation is exactly characterized by finite construction traces with a singleton base and freshness-preserving append steps. Every such construction yields a nonempty distinct collection accepted by the source operation, and every accepted collection has such a construction. Consequently, a property established for every singleton and preserved by each fresh append holds for every accepted collection, at arbitrary finite length. The converse decomposition recovers both uniqueness of the preceding collection and freshness of the last member. These results concern the exact tuple-of-string operational domain and introduce no additional scientific constructors or free parameters.

19. Complementary discrete relations

For supported R, define Rᶜ=filter_{p∉R}(P(A,B)), retaining the canonical support order. Filter membership gives
  p∈Rᶜ ⇔ p∈P(A,B) ∧ p∉R.
Consequently R and Rᶜ have no common member. Every p∈P(A,B) either belongs to R or fails its decidable finite membership test and belongs to Rᶜ. They therefore cover P(A,B). Applying the membership equation twice, with R⊆P(A,B), gives p∈(Rᶜ)ᶜ ⇔ p∈R. This establishes the involution on membership; it does not replace arbitrary held-list order by a claim of list equality.

For accepted generated source collections, the complete Cartesian pair support contains each pair once. Holding precisely the support pairs absent from a given relation produces its complement within that same support, preserving support order. The complement satisfies the original generated-relation acceptance guard. Its membership is equivalent to source-endpoint membership together with absence from the held relation; it is disjoint from the held relation, and the two cover the complete support. For an accepted relation, taking the complement twice restores its membership. This is a membership result: the source permits arbitrary held-pair order, which need not equal the canonical support order.

20. Composition of discrete maps

For R on A×B and S on B×C, the composition enumerates (a,c) for every retained occurrence (a,b)∈R linked to (b,c)∈S. Therefore
  (a,c)∈S∘R ⇔ there exists b with (a,b)∈R and (b,c)∈S.
If Out_R(a)=[(a,b_a)] and Out_S(b_a)=[(b_a,c_a)], the nested enumeration for a contains precisely [(a,c_a)]. Thus length(Out_(S∘R)(a))=1. Endpoint containment follows from a∈A and c_a∈C. With unique input pairs, two identical output occurrences would require the same unique first link and same unique second link, so no duplicate output is introduced. This derives preservation of the actual singleton-list map condition.

Relation composition retains each pair of linked occurrences through their equal intermediate endpoint. Every composed pair has witnesses in both input relations, and every linked pair is retained. For a total single-valued first relation whose targets belong to the second relation’s source, and a total single-valued second relation, the outgoing composed pair list for every original source member is a singleton. Thus composition preserves the original exact one-image predicate. When both input relations satisfy their generated-relation guards on the shared interface, the composed relation also satisfies that guard on the first source and final target: its endpoints are supported and its pairs are unique. These results preserve finite occurrence counts and require no fitted values or additional scientific parameters.

The native discrete program adapter preserves the registered program interface and its complete outputs. Fresh execution agrees with all 256 actual candidate lookups, every candidate trace and decision proof hash, the census completeness certificate, registration fields, closure and generality hashes, and all four control records. Lookup is determined by the candidate identifier, and an unknown identifier retains the source StopIteration failure. Generality serialization retains the original witness fields. These program-method correspondences supplement the native discrete derivations; full engine admission and dependency integration remain separately tracked.

21. Primitive Fold maps and ordered composition

On admitted reduced parts 0<x≤1, write I(x)=x, S(x)=x², K(x)=1 and F(x)=cast(2x). Finite word evaluation satisfies E([],x)=x and E(f::w,x)=E(w,f(x)); induction gives E(u++v,x)=E(v,E(u,x)). The identities I(x)=I(y)⇒x=y and K(x)=K(y)=1 identify the identity and constant behaviors. If S(x)=S(y), positive cross-values have equal squares. If either cross-value were strictly smaller, monotonicity of positive multiplication would make its square strictly smaller too. Both strict alternatives are impossible, so the cross-values agree and reduced-ratio identity forces x=y. For 0<x<1, multiplication by positive x preserves x<1, giving x²<x. Repeated application yields S^n(x)<x for every positive n, excluding a return; x=1 is fixed. Finally F(1/2)=F(1)=1 with 1/2≠1, while F(1/3)=2/3 and F(2/3)=1/3. Fold is therefore noninjective and has a nonstatic recurrent pair.

The declared primitive self-maps are identity, square, constant One and Fold on reduced positive exact parts through One. Native evaluation retains each original operation, including exact rational normalization for square, and executes arbitrary finite words from left to right. Concatenating two words is equivalent to executing the first followed by the second. Typed primitive words and their original string names have identical execution; unknown names retain the source ValueError. Identity is injective and fixes every input; constant One collapses every input to the fixed One. Squaring preserves its exact rational value and the admitted part domain. Fold is noninjective because half-One and One share an image, and has a nonstatic recurrent orbit through one-third and two-thirds. These results supply the primitive operational derivations; the complete least-size uniqueness argument remains separately tracked.

The square primitive is injective on the entire admitted exact-part domain. Equality of normalized squared parts implies equality of the original squared cross-products, hence equality of their positive cross-values and of the reduced parts. For every proper part, its square is strictly smaller in exact cross-product order and remains proper. Structural induction then establishes strict contraction at every positive finite iteration count. A positive-time return under square occurs if and only if the input is the fixed One, excluding nonstatic recurrent square orbits throughout the declared domain.

22. Least-size primitive generator

The declared generating predicate is noninjectivity together with nonstatic finite recurrence. At word size one, I fails noninjectivity; S fails noninjectivity and has no nonstatic return; K has only the fixed image One; F satisfies both conditions by the preceding exact identities. Hence F is the unique admitted size-one primitive. A finite word of later size has size>1, so cannot be a least-size generator when F already has size one. Appending one primitive increases size by one and preserves this exclusion. The executed prefix count is
  4¹+4²+4³=4+16+64=84,
with every ordered word retained. The argument concerns this declared four-primitive grammar and predicate.

Within the declared four-primitive ordered-word grammar, generation is represented by noninjectivity together with nonstatic finite recurrence. The native behavior proofs exclude identity, square and constant One and establish that Fold generates. Fold is therefore the unique least-size generating word. This result quantifies over every finite word, beyond the executed size-three prefix. Appending a primitive increases construction size by one and preserves the nonminimality of every later word. The complete executed prefix contains all 84 words of sizes one, two and three; each source acceptance decision is equivalent to the derived least-generator predicate. Fresh execution preserves every word, one-based rank, identifier, exact form, decision and reason. The source explicitly restricts uniqueness to the declared normalized grammar and generator predicate. Its local normalization reductions are established on the exact operational domain; unrestricted expression-language uniqueness is not asserted.

The native primitive-map program preserves all 84 actual candidate lookups, complete census and trace hashes, decision proof hashes, registration fields, closure and positive-word-size generality certificate, and original control records. The artifact control checks the full record count, actual survivor count and fourth-record identifier, and its passing result has a zero-axiom proof. Exact preimage and recurrent-orbit controls retain their original computations. Unknown candidate identifiers retain the source KeyError failure. Fresh complete program execution matches the pinned V3 artifacts.

The stated primitive normalization reductions hold for the native exact operations. Multiplication by One on either side preserves every admitted part; self-product is square and casting self-junction is Fold. Any positive Fraction whose numerator is a positive integral multiple of its denominator casts to the One. Raw self-junction is not a total self-map of admitted parts, since its value at One exceeds One. Guarded removal rejects equal operands for every admitted part and therefore is not total. These results retain the source’s declared normalized grammar and operation guards.

23. Ordered exact derivation traces

For an accepted operation o and current value x, let T_o(x) be its exact result. A constructed row records o, the ordered inputs, y=T_o(x), and the canonical identity H(y). Replay accepts the row only when its recomputed y and H(y) both equal the record. With R denoting replay, the base and successor equations are
  R([],x)=x,
  R(row(o,x)::t,x)=R(t,T_o(x))
when the recorded fields match; otherwise replay rejects. Induction proves replay of every constructed finite trace, and splitting at a prefix gives R(u++v,x)=R(v,R(u,x)) whenever the prefix succeeds. In the concrete three-move trace,
  1/3 →F 2/3 →Take(1,·) 1/3 →F 2/3.
The terminal equals the direct first Fold result, while an unregistered operation or a recorded output unequal to the recomputed value fails the corresponding replay condition.

A derivation row retains its operation name, ordered input identities, exact output and output identity. The native replay function starts at one-of-three and executes the registered Fold or guarded Take operation in source order. Each result must match both the recorded exact output and its canonical SHA256 identity. Unknown operations and mismatched outputs retain the original rejection behavior.

Constructive induction establishes that every successfully constructed finite operation sequence replays to its computed terminal value. If a recorded prefix replays to a given exact part, appending a row constructed from the next accepted operation extends replay to that operation’s exact result. Concatenation preserves replay through the intermediate terminal. These theorems have no finite-length cutoff and add no scientific parameters or axioms. They formalise the base/successor replay component of SFT-FOUNDATION-DERIVATION-TRACE-001. The registered source dependencies and complete executable evidence are verified alongside these general replay results.

The concrete three-move derivation computes Fold(one-of-three), Take(One, two-of-three), and Fold(one-of-three), retaining the exact outputs two-of-three, one-of-three, and two-of-three. Its terminal agrees with the independent direct Fold route. All ordered input identities and exact output identities are retained. Replacing the final operation with Select is rejected as unregistered. Changing the final exact output to one-of-three while preserving its other fields is rejected by replay. Native proofs establish these outcomes and the corresponding boundary control. Fresh native execution agrees with the source artifacts for all four original control records and their receipt hashes, and with independent exact arithmetic and hashing for every row and mutation.

The derivation-record grammar is the complete product of eight source axes: source custody, dependency coverage, step order, intermediate exactness, operation registration, replay equality, terminal identity and added-data status. Its 256 records retain source enumeration order, including no-extra before has-extra on the final axis. Exhaustive membership and cardinality are proved, and acceptance is equivalent to the conjunction of the eight admitted criteria. The unique survivor is the complete source-bound, dependency-complete, ordered, exact, registered, identically replayed, terminal-bound record without extra data. Each rejected axis excludes acceptance.

Fresh native program execution reproduces every candidate, every actual identifier lookup and the complete census, trace, decision, closure and generality digests. Registration, original control receipts and unknown-identifier behavior also agree with pinned V3. These classifier and program-correspondence results accompany the independent native operational replay and finite successor proofs; full engine/dependency admission remains separately tracked.

24. Finite combinatorial selections

Selections are generated by retaining the previous ordered family and appending an ordered copy with the next carrier label held at the end of each trace. A native finite construction relation records the absent and held choices at each successor. Structural induction proves that this relation describes exactly the generated raw traces, that every retained label belongs to the carrier, and that fresh-member absent and held branches are disjoint. For a unique carrier, raw selection traces are unique at every finite length. The output-trace count at a successor is the sum of the two generated branch lengths; no closed-form counting formula is introduced.

The executable output conversion replaces the empty trace with the source singleton empty-One label and preserves trace count. Selection input guards and fresh-extension guards retain their exact domains and errors. The original three-label example and its fresh-extension construction are proved in source order. Fresh Lean comparisons cover 341 selection inputs and 32 recurrence inputs, including the literal empty-One encoding. The uniqueness theorem concerns pre-conversion choice traces. Sections 29 and the subsequent carrier constructions state the precise encoding and discrete-admission results.

Write list concatenation as ++, the empty trace as [], and the ordered raw family on a carrier C as S(C). The construction is
  S([])=[[]],
  S(C++[x])=S(C) ++ [u++[x] : u∈S(C)],
where the second list retains the order of S(C). Define H(C,u) by the base H([],[]) and the two successor rules H(C,u)⇒H(C++[x],u) and H(C,u)⇒H(C++[x],u++[x]). At the base, membership in [[]] is exactly u=[]. At a successor, membership in a concatenation is membership in either branch; membership in its mapped branch means u=v++[x] for some v∈S(C). Substituting the induction hypothesis in these two cases gives
  u∈S(C) ⇔ H(C,u).
Induction on H also gives y∈u⇒y∈C. If x∉C, no old trace u can equal v++[x], since the right side contains x and containment would force x∈C. Appending [x] is injective: u++[x]=v++[x]⇒u=v. Therefore distinctness of the old family implies distinctness of each branch and disjointness between them, proving distinctness at the successor. Length of concatenation and preservation of length by mapping give
  |S(C++[x])|=|S(C)|+|S(C)|.
For C=[a,b,c], the ordered raw result is [[],[a],[b],[a,b],[c],[a,c],[b,c],[a,b,c]]. The output map e sends [] to the source empty-One trace and leaves every nonempty trace unchanged. Thus |map(e,S(C))|=|S(C)|; this count identity does not by itself assert injectivity of the encoding. The public generator admits C precisely when C≠[] and its labels are distinct.


25. Finite arrangements by held choice

The native arrangement generator retains the source carrier order: each available label is held in turn, all occurrences of that held label are removed from the recursive carrier, and the held label is prepended to every resulting suffix. Nonempty unique carriers retain the source admission guard. On this domain, each remainder removes exactly one label, remains unique, and is nonempty whenever the parent has at least two members. Recursion is justified by strictly decreasing carrier length, without a depth cutoff or an imported counting formula.

Well-founded induction proves that every generated arrangement is a permutation of the unique source carrier. Thus every output retains all original labels with their multiplicities and introduces none. Fresh Lean execution matches independently generated permutations in exact order for 64 admitted carriers, comprising 748 arrangement rows, within 341 input cases including rejected empty and repeated-label carriers. The unbounded output-permutation theorem is distinct from these finite execution checks. Converse enumeration completeness and uniqueness of the complete output family are established for every nonempty unique finite carrier.

For a held label x define R(C,x) by deleting all occurrences of x while preserving the order of all other labels:
  R([],x)=[],
  R(y::C,x)=R(C,x) if y=x, and y::R(C,x) otherwise.
Its membership law is y∈R(C,x) ⇔ (y∈C and y≠x). For distinct C and x∈C, induction on C yields
  |R(C,x)|+1=|C|,    distinct(R(C,x)).
Indeed, at the occurrence of x its removal contributes exactly one, and distinctness excludes further occurrences; before it, every retained head adds one to both lengths. If |C|≥2, the remainder is nonempty, and its smaller length justifies the recursive call.

Let A(C) be the raw arrangement family. Its source equations are
  A([])=[],    A([x])=[[x]],
  A(C)=concat, in carrier order x∈C, of [x::u : u∈A(R(C,x))] when |C|≥2.
The empty input is rejected by the public guard. Write u∼C for list permutation, retaining multiplicities. Removing the held occurrence gives C∼x::R(C,x). By the induction hypothesis u∈A(R(C,x)) implies u∼R(C,x), hence
  x::u ∼ x::R(C,x) ∼ C.
The singleton base is immediate. Well-founded induction on |C| therefore proves that every generated arrangement permutes its carrier.


26. Arrangement completeness and uniqueness

Every permutation of a nonempty unique carrier is generated by the original arrangement function. The proof takes the first label of a proposed permutation, establishes its occurrence in the carrier, and identifies the suffix as a permutation of the exact held-label remainder. Well-founded induction then supplies the recursive suffix, which the existing held-choice branch prepends in source order. Together with output preservation, this proves membership in the generated family if and only if a form permutes the carrier.

Each held-label branch is duplicate-free by recursive uniqueness and injectivity of prepending a fixed label. Different branches have distinct first labels and are disjoint. Their source-ordered concatenation is therefore duplicate-free. The combined theorem applies directly to every successful call of the guarded arrangements API: its output contains every carrier permutation exactly once. The result is uniform in finite carrier size and uses no counting formula or depth bound.

For the converse, take v∼C with C nonempty and distinct. Permutations preserve length, so v is nonempty; write v=x::u. Preservation of membership gives x∈C. Applying removal of x to the permutation gives
  R(x::u,x) ∼ R(C,x).
Distinctness is preserved by permutation, so x∉u and R(x::u,x)=u. Thus u∼R(C,x). In the nonsingleton case the remainder is admitted and shorter, and induction gives u∈A(R(C,x)); the x branch then supplies x::u∈A(C). In the singleton case equal length and membership force the sole output. Combining both directions yields
  v∈A(C) ⇔ v∼C.
For uniqueness, x::u=x::v implies u=v, so prepending preserves the recursive family's distinctness. An intersection of branches held at x and y would give x::u=y::v and hence x=y; distinct carrier labels exclude this between different branches. Consequently the ordered concatenation has no duplicate traces. For every successful guarded call arrangements(C)=ok(L),
  distinct(L) and (v∈L ⇔ v∼C).
This establishes exact enumeration and uniqueness together, for every admitted finite carrier.


27. Disjoint class accounting

For the classifier’s resulting text labels, the class ledger retains keys in first-occurrence order and appends each input occurrence to its matching class. Empty input and repeated items retain their original behavior. Native proofs establish that class insertion and complete ledger construction preserve the input family as a permutation, hence preserve every occurrence and the total count. Class keys are unique.

Lookup of any class equals the stable filter of the input by that class label. This equality preserves both multiplicity and within-class order, and implies that different labels have disjoint classes. These results hold for arbitrary finite inputs and classification functions at the source text-label boundary. Fresh Lean execution matches independent first-occurrence ordering and per-class filtering in 1,026 cases, including identity, constant and the original a/other classification witness.

Let I(L,t,x) insert the occurrence x into the ledger L under the text label t. Its equations are
  I([],t,x)=[(t,[x])],
  I((k,G)::L,t,x)=(k,G++[x])::L if k=t,
  I((k,G)::L,t,x)=(k,G)::I(L,t,x) if k≠t.
Starting with L=[], process input occurrences in their original order using t=f(x). An existing key remains in its position; a new key reaches the empty suffix and is appended. This proves first-occurrence key order and uniqueness of keys by induction.

Write F(L) for concatenation of the groups. In either insertion branch,
  F(I(L,t,x)) ∼ F(L)++[x],
where ∼ denotes permutation with multiplicity. For a matching group, moving x past the subsequent groups proves this relation; for a nonmatching group, retain that head group and apply induction to the suffix. Iterating gives F(ledger(C,f))∼C and therefore |F(ledger(C,f))|=|C|, including repeated occurrences.

For lookup Q(L,u), insertion satisfies
  Q(I(L,t,x),u)=Q(L,u)++[x] if t=u,
  Q(I(L,t,x),u)=Q(L,u) otherwise.
Induction on the input then yields Q(ledger(C,f),u)=filter(x↦f(x)=u,C). This stable filter retains precisely the original occurrences of class u in their original order. In particular x∈Q(ledger(C,f),u) iff x∈C and f(x)=u. If u≠v and an item belonged to both groups, these two equalities would give u=f(x)=v, a contradiction. This proves disjointness directly, without signed inclusion–exclusion.


28. Combinatorial witnesses and complete grammar

The five registered operational witnesses are retained: six unique ordered arrangements of three labels; exact carrier coverage of each arrangement; eight distinct selection forms including the empty One; the original a/other class ledger; and the fresh-member extension count. Each witness is evaluated from the translated operations and has a native proof. The six arrangement rows are derived through the original recursive equations in exact source order.

The combinatorial candidate grammar contains all 256 products of carrier coverage, choice rule, provenance, duplication policy, symmetry identity, class accounting, recurrence closure and extra-formula status. Native proofs establish exhaustive membership, cardinality, acceptance exactly at the all-admitted coordinate, and rejection of each inadmissible coordinate. Fresh native program execution reproduces all candidate lookups, source records, census and trace identities, decision proofs, closure and generality certificates, registration fields and four original control receipts. The general operational derivations accompany these finite grammar and program-correspondence results. The combinatorics entry is complete against its registered V3 source; the encoding results retain their precise domains as stated below.

The held-choice recursion on [a,b,c] produces, in order,
  [a,b,c], [a,c,b], [b,a,c], [b,c,a], [c,a,b], [c,b,a].
There are six distinct traces, and direct inspection of each trace gives length three and the exact label set {a,b,c}. The selection recurrence of Section 24 produces its eight listed traces, with [] encoded as the empty One. For the original classifier f(a)=a-class and f(b)=f(c)=other-class, the ledger is [(a-class,[a]),(other-class,[b,c])], whose flattening is [a,b,c]. Extending the four encoded selections on [a,b] by c retains those four and appends [c],[a,c],[b,c],[a,b,c], giving eight traces. Thus all five witness predicates follow from the same executed constructors.

For the grammar, write its eight binary coordinate domains as D₁,…,D₈ in the source order listed above. The complete ordered Cartesian product is D₁×⋯×D₈; successive two-way branching gives 2⁸=256 candidates. Its acceptance predicate is the conjunction of the eight coordinate predicates. The all-admitted tuple satisfies each conjunct. Conversely, acceptance forces each coordinate to its admitted value, so coordinate equality identifies that tuple uniquely. A rejected value in any coordinate falsifies its own conjunct. These finite grammar statements accompany the operational recurrence and enumeration proofs rather than supplying those proofs themselves.


29. Selection encoding and extension

For a raw selection form, source extension commutes with empty-form encoding exactly when that raw form differs from the singleton literal empty-One marker. A native equivalence proves both directions. For distinct admitted carriers without that literal marker, encoding preserves distinct selection forms. With a fresh label different from the marker, the original recurrence operation on encoded selections equals the original selections operation on the carrier extended by that label. These conditions are explicit theorem premises; the executable input guards remain those of v3. The source accepts the literal marker as a label, and its singleton output is proved to contain two identical encoded forms. The full-domain result uses raw ordered-choice provenance and exact encoded membership. Marker-separation remains a premise of the encoded injectivity theorem, rather than an additional executable input guard.

Let m=["empty-One"] be the literal marker trace, e([])=m and e(x::u)=x::u. Define extension X(v,z)=[z] if v=m, and v++[z] otherwise. If u=[], then X(e(u),z)=[z]=e(u++[z]).
