 \iff\bigwedge_{i=1}^{9}[c_i=\operatorname{admitted}_i]
 \iff c=c_*.
\]

Complete product membership covers every typed tuple. Equality with the original source decision is checked over that full product, and a rejected coordinate contradicts the corresponding conjunct. These grammar results accompany the six operational witnesses and general graph proofs.


66. Graph induction and locality of path updates

The One graph with no edges is admitted by the original constructor, supplies its identity path and identity reachability, and has no directed cycle. Under extensions retaining prior nodes and edges, every existing complete path and return trace remains valid. Consequently prior reachability on an admitted graph and prior detected cycles are retained.

For a carrier extended by a fresh node and an appended edge selection, any newly valid path whose nodes all belong to the old carrier must contain an added edge absent from the old relation. When each added edge has the fresh node as an endpoint and that node is absent from the old carrier, such a path cannot use an added edge. Hence the original and extended path-validation results are exactly equal on every sequence of old nodes, including rejected sequences. This establishes the locality of path updates in the source fresh-node induction step.

These results retain the explicit conditions of the extension. They do not impose new constructor guards or restrict the original graph operations. Ordered pair-support generation, cut and paired-balance extension are established below; held-selection characterization is also established below. These are results of the completed graph entry.


The base graph is G₁ = ([One],[]), with Valid([One]) = true, Reachable(One,One) = true and CycleExists(G₁) = false. If V ⊆ V' and E ⊆ E', every valid old path retains all node and edge checks. Every old return trace likewise retains each edge and held-prefix comparison. The path and cycle equivalences therefore transfer positive reachability and cycle results.

For V' = V ++ [z] and E' = E ++ A, suppose a sequence P contains only old nodes, is rejected by G and accepted by G'. The accepted path is nonempty and satisfies all node checks already in V. Hence some adjacency must account for the change:

\[
 \exists e\in\operatorname{Adj}(P),\quad e\in A\land e\notin E.
\]

Now assume z ∉ V and every edge of A touches z. Both endpoints of every adjacency in P belong to P and hence to V. No such adjacency can touch z, contradicting the displayed witness. Thus

\[
 \forall P\text{ with nodes in }V,\quad
 \operatorname{Valid}_{(V\mathbin{+\!+}[z],E\mathbin{+\!+}A)}(P)
 =\operatorname{Valid}_{(V,E)}(P).
\]

The equality includes rejected and empty sequences. It follows from the explicit fresh-endpoint condition, without adding a restriction to the original path validator.


67. Admitted graph extension, ordered pairs, cuts and balance

The graph constructor succeeds exactly when its node list is nonempty and distinct, its edge list is distinct, and every endpoint belongs to the node list. If a genuinely fresh node is appended, a distinct added-edge selection whose endpoints lie in the enlarged carrier and whose edges touch that fresh node is disjoint from the old relation. The combined graph therefore satisfies precisely the original constructor checks.

The complete ordered node-pair product extends row by row: each old left-node row retains all old right-node cells followed by its fresh-right cell; the final fresh-left row contains every old right node followed by the fresh self-pair. The equality retains the full generated order and does not assert that every generated pair is selected as an edge.

For an already successful held cut selection, extending the node carrier and appending edges returns exactly the old cut list followed by the added edges whose endpoint held-membership differs. Selection validity follows from the original nonempty and carrier-membership guard; no distinctness requirement is added to the held list. For an internally balanced pair of ingress/egress trace lists, appending one previously absent trace to each side preserves equal counts and separate distinctness, hence preserves the original balance predicate.


Let G = (V,E) be admitted, z ∉ V, and A a distinct added-edge list whose endpoints lie in V' = V ++ [z] and whose every edge touches z. If e belonged to both E and A, an old endpoint would equal z, contradicting z ∉ V. Thus E and A are disjoint, their concatenation is distinct, and all constructor guards hold for G' = (V',E ++ A).

Writing row(u,V) = [(u,v):v ∈ V] as an ordered list, the full pair-support identity is

\[
 \operatorname{Pairs}(V')=
 \mathop{\mathrm{concat}}_{u\in V}(\operatorname{row}(u,V)\mathbin{+\!+}[(u,z)])
 \mathbin{+\!+}(\operatorname{row}(z,V)\mathbin{+\!+}[(z,z)]).
\]

This distributes map and flatMap over append and retains each old row's order. For a previously successful cut with fixed held H, filter distribution gives

\[
 \operatorname{Cut}_H(E\mathbin{+\!+}A)
 =\operatorname{Cut}_H(E)\mathbin{+\!+}
 [e\in A\mid(e_1\in H)\mathbin{\mathrm{xor}}(e_2\in H)].
\]

For balanced ingress and egress lists I,O and fresh respective traces i,o,

\[
 |I\mathbin{+\!+}[i]|=|I|+1=|O|+1=|O\mathbin{+\!+}[o]|.
\]

Fresh append preserves each list's distinctness, and the incoming list remains nonempty, proving the original balance predicate after extension.


68. Held-partition updates and exact paired-balance conditions

Appending a carrier node to a valid held selection replaces each endpoint-membership test by membership in the old selection or in that appended singleton. Filtering the complete original edge list by the resulting unequal-membership test gives exactly the updated cut, including the original allowance for repeated held nodes. An edge whose endpoints differ from the appended node has an unchanged cut decision.

For an admitted old graph, a genuinely fresh node belongs to neither endpoint of any old edge. If that node is appended to both carrier and held selection while new edges are appended, the resulting cut is exactly the prior cut followed by the added edges crossing the enlarged held partition. The equation preserves original list order.

Appending one ingress trace and one egress trace yields internal balance if and only if the original trace counts agree, each original trace list is distinct, and neither appended trace already occurs on its respective side. This characterization includes empty original lists: their first trace pair is balanced. For an already balanced pair, the exact remaining requirements are freshness on both sides. No restriction on trace contents is added to the original operation.


Define the endpoint indicator χ_H(x) by membership in the held list. Appending y gives χ_(H++[y])(x) = χ_H(x) ∨ (x=y). Therefore the updated cut is exactly

\[
 [e\in E\mid(\chi_H(e_1)\lor e_1=y)
 \mathbin{\mathrm{xor}}(\chi_H(e_2)\lor e_2=y)].
\]

If neither endpoint is y, both added equalities are false and the old cut decision is unchanged. When y = z is fresh to an admitted old graph, this applies to every old edge. For H' = H ++ [z], the combined carrier/edge extension consequently satisfies

\[
 \operatorname{Cut}_{H'}(E\mathbin{+\!+}A)
 =\operatorname{Cut}_{H}(E)\mathbin{+\!+}\operatorname{Cut}_{H'}(A),
\]

where the rightmost term denotes the same ordered crossing-edge filter. Repeated members in H affect neither the indicator nor the source's valid-selection guard.

The exact paired-balance equivalence is

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \operatorname{Balance}(I\mathbin{+\!+}[i],O\mathbin{+\!+}[o])\iff{}&
 |I|=|O|\land\operatorname{Distinct}(I)\land\operatorname{Distinct}(O)\\
 &\land i\notin I\land o\notin O.
 \end{aligned}
\]

The appended incoming list is automatically nonempty; equal successor lengths cancel to |I| = |O|, and distinctness of each append is exactly prior distinctness plus absence of the new trace. In particular Balance([i],[o]) = true for arbitrary traces i,o, including empty traces, because trace contents are unrestricted by this operation.

77. General shortest-path and reachability derivations

The search queue preserves nondecreasing path lengths and a width of at most one edge. When a node is visited, every adjacent node that remains unvisited has a queued route bounded by any path to that visited node followed by the edge. This invariant, together with the retained origin route, supplies a frontier route no longer than any proposed trace to an unvisited endpoint. Since the target is returned upon discovery and is never queued, the final extension is no longer than any alternative trace to that target.

For every finite source edge list and distinct admitted endpoints, every undirected trace therefore yields a successful returned path of no greater length. Combined with returned-path soundness, this proves minimality of the actual result against all finite undirected alternatives. The procedure returns None if and only if no such trace exists. The proof retains the original carrier guard and does not require unique edges, globally admitted edge endpoints, or exclusion of loops. Equal endpoints retain the separate source identity result, empty One. These statements are general native proofs, with zero axiom dependencies; they do not depend on a finite regression bound.

320. Squared Planck/proton support and terminal transport

Write c=3 for the derived generator and b=2 for the derived binary count. The successor volume and its least cover are
  V = c^(c+1) = 3^4 = 81,
  d = min{k ∈ ℕ : k>0 and 81 ≤ 2^k} = 7.
Indeed 2^6=64<81≤128=2^7; monotonicity gives 2^k≤64 for every positive k<7. The complete support is N=b^d=128. Its positive predecessor m satisfies m+1=N, hence m=127 uniquely. Consequently the squared hierarchy is H²=b^m=2^127, and the corresponding unsquared exponent is m/2=127/2. These are successive operations on the derived counts; no exponent is independently selected.

For terminal transport let α=3676744786/503846395469 and g_c=2/3. The removed share and retained complement are
  g_c α = 7353489572/1511539186407,
  t = 1−g_c α = 1504185696835/1511539186407.
The inequalities 0<7353489572<1511539186407 imply 0<t<1. Multiplication of the bare squared carrier by this complement gives
  H_terminal² = 2^127 t
             = 255923934603817488008405160690199418432572494970880/1511539186407.
Thus 0<H_terminal²<H². This equation describes the terminal component; completion of that separate source entry is recorded separately from completion of the base hierarchy.

Generator-three successor volume is 81, whose least binary cover depth is 7. Complete support has 128 positions and its unique positive predecessor has 127 massive positions. The retained unsquared exponent is 127/2; the exact squared comparison carrier is 2^127. Native proofs retain the least-cover exclusion, predecessor uniqueness and both exact exponent records.

Terminal transport uses the already derived electromagnetic share 3676744786/503846395469 and the colour share 2/3. The retained positive One-complement multiplies the bare hierarchy once, producing 255923934603817488008405160690199418432572494970880/1511539186407. The native result preserves the strict-positive-retained-share guard and the lower squared hierarchy.

322. Complete prime-sector construction

Using the least cover d=7 derived above, define
  P = {p ∈ {2,3,4,5,6,7} : ∀a ∈ {2,…,p−1}, a does not divide p}.

The excluded counts have explicit divisors: 4=2·2 and 6=2·3. Therefore P={2,3,5,7}, with every count in the source range decided. For each admitted p, complete squared support and its positive predecessor give
  M(p)+1=p²,  M(p)=p²−1,
  g(p)=(p−1)/p.
Evaluation in source order yields
  (M(2),M(3),M(5),M(7))=(3,8,24,48),
  (g(2),g(3),g(5),g(7))=(1/2,2/3,4/5,6/7).
Although 11 is prime, 11>7, so it is outside this derived sector support.

323. Exact Fold return and binary multiplicative order

For q>0, let h_q(x) be the held representative of x modulo q: h_q(x)=x mod q when that remainder is nonzero, and h_q(x)=q otherwise. The executed recurrence is
  r_0=p,  r_(k+1)=h_q(2r_k).
Induction gives r_k ≡ p·2^k (mod q): the initial step is p≡p·1, and doubling the induction identity gives r_(k+1)≡2p·2^k=p·2^(k+1). For a reduced input, gcd(p,q)=1. Because held representatives are unique in {1,…,q},
  r_k=p ⇔ p·2^k≡p (mod q)
        ⇔ q divides p(2^k−1)
        ⇔ q divides 2^k−1
        ⇔ 2^k≡1 (mod q).
The cancellation uses gcd(p,q)=1. Taking the least positive k on both sides identifies first Fold return with first binary multiplicative order whenever that return exists. For odd q>1, existence follows from the finite-carrier argument below; the even-denominator transient is treated separately.

For every supplied positive reduced part p/q through the One, native repeated double-and-cast execution satisfies rank_k modulo q = p times 2^k modulo q. The complete-denominator representative is retained when a remainder vanishes. Reducedness permits cancellation of p: an exact return to the original held rank occurs if and only if 2^k modulo q equals 1 modulo q. The first-positive-return and first-positive-order predicates therefore have the same least index. These identities hold at arbitrary supplied finite depth; they do not use a bounded prime list. All 25 definitions and supporting theorems have zero Lean axiom dependencies.

Successful finite witness replays preserve the original order-loop and orbit-loop guard, update and stopping branches. The native correspondence proofs connect returned replay records to those source loop relations. 325. Complete cyclotomic counting and source-loop termination

For odd q>1, gcd(2,q)=1. If two powers at indices i<j have the same residue, cancellation of 2^i gives 2^(j−i)≡1 (mod q). The q+1 residues at indices 0,…,q cannot all be distinct in a carrier of q residues, so a positive return exists. Let ℓ be the least such index. For a reduced starting rank p, an equality r_i=r_j with 0≤i<j<ℓ would imply 2^(j−i)≡1, contradicting minimality. Hence its complete cycle C(p) has exactly ℓ members.

For any cycles C(p), C(s), suppose r_i(p)=r_j(s). Each cycle has period ℓ, so advancing within the cycle by the complementary index ℓ−j expresses s as an iterate of p. The same reasoning expresses p as an iterate of s. Thus C(p)=C(s). Every reduced rank lies in its own cycle, and distinct cycles are disjoint. If R_q={r:1≤r<q, gcd(r,q)=1} and C is the number of cycles, successive removal of complete cycles therefore gives
  |R_q| = ℓ C.
For prime q, each 1≤r<q is coprime to q, so |R_q|=q−1 and
  q−1 = ℓ C, hence ℓ divides q−1.
This is a finite partition derivation for each admitted denominator, independent of the finite witness table.

For every admitted odd denominator above the One, the finite carrier forces a first binary return. If no return occurred through the carrier bound, one more executed position than there are held ranks would have to remain distinct, contradicting the exact finite count. The original order loop therefore terminates, and its returned count is the first binary multiplicative order. For every reduced numerator, the original orbit loop returns a complete ordered trace of exactly this length.

The executed cycles preserve reducedness. Two cycles that share a rank have identical members; every reduced rank belongs to its own cycle. Removing complete cycles successively from the full reduced-residue support proves that the residue count equals orbit length times orbit count. For a prime denominator, every positive rank below the prime is reduced, so the period divides one less than the prime. These are arbitrary supplied finite-denominator results, not conclusions extrapolated from the witness census.

The orbit loop also terminates for positive held parts with even denominators: each fresh step adds a distinct held rank, and the finite carrier bounds the number of additions before repetition. Combined with the counted binary-depth result, this retains the transient prefix before the odd recurrent core. The new derivation batch has 71 audited definitions and theorems with zero axiom dependencies; the original 256-form program batch has a further 108.

327. Exact positive-ratio composition

Let N(a,b) denote division of a positive numerator and denominator by their greatest common divisor. Its normalized pair (u,v) satisfies av=ub. For an arbitrary reduced positive ratio x=(n,d), define its reciprocal y=N(d,n) and composition x·y=N(ny_n,dy_d). Value preservation for y gives dy_d=y_n n=ny_n. The composite therefore has equal positive raw numerator and denominator, and normalization gives (1,1). Every admitted positive ratio composed with its reciprocal returns the One, including ratios above the One.

For positive counts a,b,c, the raw product of the views a/b and b/c is N(ab,bc)=(u,v). Value preservation gives abv=ubc. Associativity and commutativity place the common positive factor b on the same side; applying finite division by b and the identity (bk)/b=k gives av=uc. Thus this composite represents a/c exactly. For any positive m, the raw product of m/1 and 1/m is (m·1,1·m); commutativity makes the sides equal, and normalization yields (1,1). This reconstructs the One from m corresponding equal parts.

These native component results have thirteen audited definitions and theorems with zero axiom dependencies. The full source entry additionally includes shorter separation, complement, local transport and finite-family cycle composition.

The same telescoping law holds for actual composition after normalizing both input views. Let x=N(a,b), y=N(c,d), and z=compose(x,y). Value preservation gives ax_d=x_n b, cy_d=y_n d, and (x_n y_n)z_d=z_n(x_d y_d). Put e=x_d y_d>0. Multiplying and rearranging the first two identities gives (ac)e=(x_n y_n)(bd). Multiplication by z_d and substitution of the third identity therefore yield e(ac z_d)=e(z_n bd). Exact positive cancellation gives ac z_d=z_n bd. Specializing the second view to N(b,c) gives ab z_d=z_n bc; cancellation of b gives a z_d=z_n c. Thus normalization of either input and the composite preserves the telescoping relation itself.

328. Unique positive holding complement

For a generated count m>1, write m=k+2 and define h=N(k+1,k+2), the exact (m−1)-of-m part. A positive ratio x=n/d is the holding complement of the equal part 1/m precisely when x+1/m=1. Clearing the positive denominator dm gives the entirely positive-side equation
  nm+d=dm.
Expanding dm=d(k+1)+d and removing the same terminal d from both sides forces nm=(k+1)d. Normalization of h gives h_n m=(k+1)h_d, so h satisfies the holding equation. If any other positive ratio x satisfies it, multiply its forced equality by h_d and the equality for h by d. Rearranging gives m(nh_d)=m(h_n d). Cancellation by positive m yields nh_d=h_n d, establishing uniqueness of the exact rational value. At m=2, h=N(1,2)=(1,2), the half-One witness. The condition m>1 is precisely the positive-complement domain: at m=1 the holding equation would require n+d=d, which admits no positive numerator. These seven native definitions and theorems have zero axiom dependencies; the source entry's other relation and cycle requirements remain separate.

330. Positive local separation before casting

After a common finite refinement, let the held ordered positions be l/d and u/d, where 0<l<u≤d. Their positive directed gap is N(u−l,d). If this normalized pair is (g_n,g_d), then (u−l)g_d=g_n d, preserving the exact common-refinement value. The uncast Fold step doubles both held numerators. Its gap numerator is therefore
  (u+u)−(l+l)=2u−2l=2(u−l)=(u−l)+(u−l).
Normalizing the two equal positive numerators against the same denominator gives identical positive Fraction objects. For the resulting transported pair (t_n,t_d),
  2(u−l)t_d=t_n d.
Thus the uncast local gap transports by the generated binary factor. This statement concerns the local gap before whole-turn casting; it does not identify every directed gap with the shorter circular separation or omit a change of cast branch. The component's seven audited declarations have no axiom dependencies.

For two positions on the same Fold branch, the actual casting operation preserves this doubled gap. If 2u≤d, then also 2l≤d; both cast numerators are unchanged, and their difference is 2(u−l). If d<2l, then d<2u and both doubled numerators are at most 2d. Casting therefore removes exactly d from each. The retained difference is (2u−d)−(2l−d)=2u−2l=2(u−l). The shared-removal identity follows by restoring d to both retained numerators and cancelling the common added term. Thus both same-branch cases are connected to actual complete-One removal, not only to uncast arithmetic. Branch-crossing shorter-separation classification is a separate part of the relation derivation. The local component now has eleven audited declarations without axiom dependencies.

331. Least common return of every positive finite cycle family

For supplied positive periods a,b, let g=gcd(a,b). The actual native least-common-multiple operation is L=ab/g. Since g divides both a and b, exact division yields L=a(b/g)=b(a/g). Thus a divides L, b divides L, and L>0. Also gL=ab.

To prove minimality, let c>0 be any shared return, so a divides c and b divides c. Then ab divides both ac and bc. The constructive greatest-common-divisor property gives ab dividing gcd(ac,bc). Positive scaling gives gcd(ac,bc)=c·gcd(a,b)=cg. Therefore cg=abk=gLk for a finite count k. Cancelling the positive g gives c=Lk, so L divides every positive shared return and L≤c. This establishes the first common return, not merely one common multiple.

For a nonempty supplied finite family, retain its first period p and the remaining finite list P. Define J(p,[])=p and J(p,q::P)=J(lcm(p,q),P). Induction proves positivity and the exact characterization, for every positive n,
  J(p,P) divides n ⇔ p divides n and every period in P divides n.
The empty-tail case is the single cycle. At a successor, divisibility by lcm(p,q) is equivalent to simultaneous divisibility by p and q, by the two-period result; the induction hypothesis handles the remaining list. Applying the characterization to n=J(p,P) shows that every supplied period returns there. Applying it to any other positive shared return n shows J(p,P) divides n and hence J(p,P)≤n. The original witness is J(2,[3,4])=12. All sixteen native definitions and theorems in this derivation have zero axiom dependencies; the construction uses no completed infinite family or fixed period table.

332. Held orientation and exact shorter-part selection

In the first case s≤b and s=a; in the second case n_b d_a<n_a d_b, so s≤a and s=b. Hence s belongs to the supplied pair, remains positive and is no longer than either member. The held side is retained as a separate label. Changing that label changes neither the cross-products nor s, so orientation introduces no signed magnitude. In the tie case n_a d_b=n_b d_a, either presentation has the same exact ratio. The native shorter-part component proves these statements without axioms. 335. Exact finite-difference identities

Put q=2^k and h=1/q at a positive generated depth k. For the centered quadratic construction, clearing the denominator q² gives
  (q+1)²+(q−1)²=2q²+2.
Thus (1+h)²+(1−h)²=2+2h², and the centered second difference divided by h² is exactly 2. The native proof establishes the denominator identity for q=m+1 by distributivity and exact addition; it is not inferred from the finite witness list.

For the forward cubic construction, clearing q³ gives
  (q+2)³+q³=2(q+1)³+6q+6.
Equivalently (1+2h)³+1=2(1+h)³+6h²+6h³. Its forward second difference divided by h² is therefore 6+6h, with exact positive error E_k=6/2^k. The native error carrier is the normalized positive fraction N(6,2^k); its numerator and denominator satisfy 6E_d=E_n2^k. The denominator successor is 2^(k+1)=2^k+2^k. These seven audited component declarations have zero axiom dependencies.

For normalized E_k=(a_n,a_d) and E_(k+1)=(b_n,b_d), value preservation gives 6a_d=a_n2^k and 6b_d=b_n(2^k+2^k). Multiplying these identities by the opposite denominators and cancelling the positive 2^k yields (b_n+b_n)a_d=a_n b_d. Therefore 2E_(k+1)=E_k exactly. Since b_n a_d>0, it follows that b_n a_d<a_n b_d, proving strict decrease in the actual normalized representation.

There is also a finite certificate for every supplied positive rational tolerance a/d. Induction gives n+1≤2^n: at the successor, n+2≤2(n+1)≤2·2^n. Choose the finite depth K=6d. Then 2^K≥K+1>6d, so E_K=6/2^K<1/d≤a/d. The native proof multiplies through by the positive denominators and cancels only positive factors, establishing E_n d<a E_d for the normalized error pair. K is a sufficient generated certificate depth computed from the supplied tolerance; it is not a fitted scientific parameter or a claim of optimal depth. Thus the error can be certified below every supplied positive rational tolerance without introducing zero as an attained error or an ungenerated limit value. The finite-difference component now has eleven audited declarations with zero axiom dependencies.

343. Complete labelled electroweak channel supports

Generate the finite positive position list P₀=[] and P_(n+1)=P_n++[n+1]. Induction gives |P_n|=n and x∈P_n iff 0<x≤n. Every previous position is at most n, so n+1 is fresh; induction therefore proves that P_n is duplicate-free. These are labels for the counted support positions.

For finite lists X,Y, generate their complete product by concatenating [(x,y):y∈Y] for each x∈X in order. A pair belongs to this list exactly when its two coordinates belong to X and Y. Prepending each new first-coordinate block adds |Y| records, giving |X×Y|=|X|·|Y| by induction on X. Within a block, pair equality implies equality of second coordinates; between blocks, it implies equality of first coordinates. Thus distinct X and Y give a duplicate-free product.

The charged support is the full product P₁₅×P₁₅, with 15·15=225 distinct records. The neutral support is P₄×(P₁₄×P₁₄), with 4·14·14=784 distinct records, retaining each of the four neutral-channel labels and both axis positions. The native proof identifies these list lengths with the charged/neutral pair returned by the original source-count calculation. The two supports therefore supply the previously derived 225/(225+784)=225/1009 share through complete labelled enumeration. Sixteen component declarations have zero axiom dependencies.

344. General odd-denominator vacuum recurrence

For any supplied denominator q > 1 with q mod 2 = 1, let r_k be the held rank obtained after k executions of the original binary Fold from unit rank r_0 = 1. The existing exact rank calculation gives

\[
 0<r_k\le q,\qquad r_k\equiv 2^k\pmod q,\qquad
 \gcd(r_k,q)=1.
\]

The first inequality follows from the positive held-rank transition. Reducedness follows from gcd(1,q) = 1 and coprimality of every binary power with odd q. Exact held-rank recovery identifies return with the binary order condition:

\[
 r_k=1\iff 2^k\bmod q=1\bmod q.
\]

If no positive return occurred through q steps, cancellation of the common binary factor would make all q+1 positions r_0,…,r_q distinct. They lie in the complete q-member positive rank carrier, contradicting its finite cardinality. Ascending search therefore supplies a first period p with

\[
 0<p\le q,\qquad r_p=1,\qquad
 \forall j,\ 0<j<p\Longrightarrow r_j\ne1.
\]

The executed transition then gives r_(p+k) = r_k. Repeated removal of a complete period yields

\[
 r_k=r_{k\bmod p}\qquad(k\ge0).
\]

Thus positivity and exact recurrence persist at every finite continuation, without a cutoff on denominators or Fold depth. Each generated odd successor q = 2n+3 satisfies q > 1 and q mod 2 = 1 by the exact remainder identity (3+2n) mod 2 = 3 mod 2 = 1, so the same first-return theorem applies for every supplied n. Five native theorems establish these statements with no axiom dependencies. The executable odd-denominator wrapper and its complete registered entry checks are separate from these general recurrence theorems.


345. Exact odd-denominator orbit interface and returned traces

The executable recurrence interface retains the original guard order. Boolean inputs and integer denominators d ≤ 1 receive the positive-denominator error. On the remaining domain the derived binary count is two, and the next test is exactly

\[
 \lfloor d/2\rfloor\,2=d.
\]

Equality rejects the denominator as even; otherwise the source unit part 1/d is passed to the original first-return loop. Its result retains every successive Fold, with the returned source included as the last member. The final guard rejects an empty trace or an element outside the positive One domain. For every represented Part p, its stored inequalities give 0 < numerator(p) ≤ denominator(p); induction on a trace therefore proves that its outside-domain test is false. Consequently every nonempty successful trace is preserved unchanged by this final guard.

The exact computed traces include

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 d=3 &: (2/3,1/3),\\
 d=5 &: (2/5,4/5,3/5,1/5),\\
 d=7 &: (2/7,4/7,1/7),\\
 d=9 &: (2/9,4/9,8/9,7/9,5/9,1/9).
 \end{aligned}
\]

Their lengths are respectively 2,4,3,6, with each ordered equality proved by native computation. Twenty-one interface definitions and theorems have no axiom dependencies. Fresh Lean execution agrees with the unchanged pinned V3 function and its original dependencies on 36 cases: both Boolean inputs and every integer from −2 through 31, comparing the full ordered rational traces and exact rejection outcomes. This finite comparison complements the general recurrence derivation of section 344; it does not replace that derivation.


346. Exact Fraction and held-rank recurrence correspondence

Let c_q(n) denote the original positive cast: repeatedly subtract q while the retained numerator is greater than q. Each subtraction preserves the remainder, so induction on the actual removal trace proves

\[
 c_q(n)\bmod q=n\bmod q.
\]

For positive n and q, the cast lies in 1,…,q. The held-rank Fold is

\[
 F_q(n)=\begin{cases}q,&2n\bmod q=0,\\2n\bmod q,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}
\]

It lies in the same held-rank interval and has remainder 2n mod q. Two ranks in 1,…,q with equal remainders are equal: a nonzero remainder names the rank itself, while zero names the retained whole q. Hence

\[
 c_q(n+n)=F_q(n).
\]

Write N(n,q) for the original normalized positive Fraction n/q. The already derived normalization and Fold-cast identity now yields the exact state equation

\[
 \operatorname{Fold}(N(n,q))=N(c_q(n+n),q)=N(F_q(n),q).
\]

For the unit orbit define x_k = N(r_k,q), where r_k is the actual held-rank iteration from section 344. Then Fold(x_k) = x_(k+1). The rank return r_p = 1 supplied for every odd q > 1 therefore gives x_p = x_0. This is equality of the actual normalized Fraction states, including their positive-cast convention, rather than only equality of modular residues. Eight native declarations establish this correspondence and Fraction-return existence with no axiom dependencies. The original loop's first-return branch and successful output for arbitrary odd q follow from the visited-state argument below.


347. General success of the original odd-denominator first-return loop

For odd q, reducedness gives gcd(r_k,q) = 1. Normalization therefore leaves the numerator unchanged:

\[
 \operatorname{numerator}(x_k)=r_k/\gcd(r_k,q)=r_k.
\]

Equality of Fraction states implies equality of their held ranks. Before the first binary period p, the rank-cancellation proof excludes every collision at distinct positions, hence

\[
 0\le i<j<p\quad\Longrightarrow\quad x_i\ne x_j.
\]

At loop position k < p, retain the invariant that the current state is x_k and every stored visited key is the key of some x_i with i ≤ k. The actual next Fold is x_(k+1). If k+1 = p, this state is x_0, so the original return-to-source branch succeeds before testing visited keys. If it differs from the source, k+1 < p. A visited-key match would then give x_(k+1) = x_i with i ≤ k, contradicting the displayed collision exclusion. Thus the non-source-repeat error branch is impossible. Advancing adds exactly the next key and preserves the invariant. Induction over the terminating original Run proves successful output.

The retained first-return theorem supplies the complete trace structure

\[
 \operatorname{firstReturn}(x_0)=\operatorname{ok}(T),\qquad
 T=B\mathbin{+\!+}[x_0],\qquad
 \operatorname{Steps}(x_0,T),\qquad
 \forall x\in B,\ x\ne x_0.
\]

This also proves T is nonempty. For odd q, the even-denominator guard cannot hold: if floor(q/2)·2 = q, taking remainders gives q mod 2 = 0, contradicting q mod 2 = 1. The wrapper therefore reaches the original loop, and its final positive-domain guard preserves T by section 345. For every supplied n with (n+2) mod 2 = 1,

\[
 \exists T,\quad
 \operatorname{oddDenominatorOrbit}(n+2)=\operatorname{ok}(T)
 \land\operatorname{FirstReturn}(1/(n+2),T).
\]

Nine native theorems establish the Fraction-state collision exclusion, actual loop success, guard passage and complete wrapper result with no axiom dependencies. The theorem is quantified over the full admitted odd-denominator domain.


348. Original vacuum recurrence witnesses

The four original witness records use the actual odd-denominator orbit function. The first requires the ordered trace (2/3,1/3); the next two require trace lengths four and three for denominators five and seven. The fourth checks every denominator d = 2n+3 for n = 0,…,14 and requires that the final returned Fraction is 1/d:

\[
 \bigwedge_{n=0}^{14}
 \operatorname{last}(\operatorname{oddDenominatorOrbit}(2n+3))=1/(2n+3).
\]

All four predicates evaluate to true in native Lean, retaining their original names and statements. The complete prefix gives the following denominator/period pairs:

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 &(3,2),(5,4),(7,3),(9,6),(11,10),\\
 &(13,12),(15,4),(17,8),(19,18),(21,6),\\
 &(23,11),(25,20),(27,18),(29,28),(31,5).
 \end{aligned}
\]

These periods are lengths of the full ordered traces, whose final elements are respectively 1/d. Fresh execution matches those traces against the original V3 function. Ten witness definitions and theorems have no axiom dependencies. The general result for arbitrary odd denominators is the theorem of section 347; this complete finite prefix preserves the source's additional concrete witnesses.
